        Idea:
# brief-unify-capacity-control-20260513

**Profile:** `all-codex/standard/high`
**Repo:** `reigh-worker-orchestrator` (Python, Railway-deployed) + small cross-repo touch in `reigh-app/scripts/debug.py`
**Branch base:** `origin/main` at `fcec14f`
**Rollout:** shadow-mode → divergence audit → flag-flip cutover

> **Brief revision history:** drafted 2026-05-13. Sense-checked by Codex GPT-5.5 `high` (read-only, repo-aware). Corrections from that review folded in below — most importantly: code-level claims about `calculate_scaling_decision_pure` and the cycle phase order have been corrected against ground truth.

---

## Goal

Replace the orchestrator's two-controller scaling loop with one durable-intent reconciler. Specifically:

1. Introduce `worker_capacity_intents` (Postgres) as the durable source of truth for "what capacity should exist right now, for what reason, with what cooldowns."
2. Build `gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_reconciler.py` with an explicit five-phase state machine: **OBSERVE → PLAN → ADMIT → ACT → RECORD**.
3. Delete `_handle_early_termination` entirely. Its job becomes a `cancel_pending_spawn` action emitted by the reconciler under strictly-defined conditions.
4. Fold RunPod `spawn_failed` backoff into the same reconciler as admission control (no separate guardrail path).
5. Delete the test-only duplicate code path under `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` (which is *not* the production runtime path — see "Context" below).
6. Roll out via shadow mode: new reconciler runs alongside the existing controller, logs intended actions, doesn't take effect until a feature flag flips.

Success is measured by **production effective-utilisation rising from ~4% to ≥70%** sustained over 24h post-cutover, with no user-task failures attributable to the new controller.

---

## Context — what's broken

A 2-hour rollout window on 2026-05-13 observed **73 GPU workers spawned, 70 killed before becoming productive, 1 worker did all the actual work** (4% effective utilisation). The same pattern is present in git history going back at least 6 weeks.

Two parallel Codex GPT-5.5 sense-checks (independent runs, no cross-talk) reading `gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py` converged on a single architectural diagnosis, **subsequently verified by a third Codex `high` review** against the actual code:

> The orchestrator runs **two independent capacity controllers** in one cycle. `_handle_early_termination` (around `control_loop.py:340`, `current_capacity` derived at `:362`, gate check at `:377`) computes one `desired` figure from `task_counts.total * scale_down_multiplier` + min GPUs + idle buffer. Later in the same cycle, `_calculate_scaling_decision` calls `calculate_scaling_decision_pure` at `control_loop.py:802` (when `use_new_scaling_logic` is enabled), which emits spawn action math from queued coverage (`workers_covering_tasks = idle + spawning`, `uncovered_tasks = queued - workers_covering_tasks`, `spawn_for_tasks = uncovered_tasks`, see `worker_state.py:588`) plus a busy-worker buffer floor (`worker_state.py:594`). **However**, `desired_workers` itself is still populated from the legacy `total_workload * scale_up_multiplier`, `busy + idle buffer`, and `min_active_gpus` block at `worker_state.py:616`.
>
> The bug is **not** "stale snapshot between phases" — the scaling decision is computed *after* early-term, not before. The bug is that early-term **mutates the worker list** (terminating spawning workers) before a second, separate scaling formula runs against the same phase-1 task snapshot and the now-changed worker list. Two formulas, two notions of `desired`, one cycle, opposing actions.

Three named failure modes ride on this defect:

- **Spawn churn from snapshot gates.** `_handle_early_termination` is guarded by `if task_counts.queued > 0` (at `control_loop.py:377`). `task_counts.queued` is an instantaneous snapshot taken once at the top of each cycle. When the single productive worker had just claimed a segment (queue momentarily empty, six in-progress segments active), early-term fired and killed the spawning replacement. Next cycle re-spawned. Loop.
- **No backoff for RunPod `spawn_failed`.** 55 of the 73 deaths were RunPod refusing pod allocation. The orchestrator retried at 30-second cycle cadence indefinitely.
- **Mutating actions sequenced before mismatched re-derivation.** Early-term terminates spawning workers from the worker list, then `_calculate_scaling_decision` re-derives `desired` against the same phase-1 task snapshot but a now-shorter worker list, and decides to re-spawn what early-term just killed.

A team-authored postmortem (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/docs/scaling-churn-postmortem-20260513.md`) and approach memo (`/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/docs/scaling-churn-approach-20260513.md`) cover the symptom-level analysis. The architectural prescription below is the *deep* fix.

### Duplicate code path — clarified

`gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py` is **the** production runtime path. `main.py` imports `OrchestratorControlLoop` from it. The `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` mixin tree contains duplicated chunks of capacity logic (including a duplicate `_handle_early_termination`) but is **only exercised by tests, not by production**. Default deletion target for this sprint: the mixin duplication under `control/phases/`. The planner may override that choice only by first showing concretely that the mixins contain logic worth migrating into `control_loop.py` (or by proposing to flip production over to the mixin path — but that is out of scope for this sprint and would be a separate brief).

---

## Architecture — what we're building

### New Postgres table

```sql
CREATE TABLE public.worker_capacity_intents (
  id                          uuid PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(),
  pool                        text NOT NULL,
  route_key                   text,                              -- nullable for pool-wide intents
  desired_capacity            int  NOT NULL,
  reason                      text NOT NULL,                     -- 'queued_coverage'|'min_floor'|'cooldown'|...
  observed_queued             int,
  observed_active             int,
  observed_spawning           int,
  cycle_id                    bigint,
  created_at                  timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
  valid_until                 timestamptz,                       -- intent expires; absence ≠ kill
  stable_since                timestamptz,                       -- when desired_capacity last changed
  consecutive_spawn_failures  int  NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
  next_spawn_allowed_at       timestamptz,                       -- backoff cooldown
  shadow                      boolean NOT NULL DEFAULT true      -- shadow vs authoritative mode
);

CREATE INDEX worker_capacity_intents_pool_route_created
  ON public.worker_capacity_intents (pool, route_key, created_at DESC);
```

**One row written per cycle per pool**, regardless of whether the intent changed. This is the audit trail.

**Migration location:** `reigh-worker-orchestrator/sql/` (alongside the existing `2025*_*.sql` files). Filename pattern: `20260514000000_create_worker_capacity_intents.sql`. The repo's existing migration registration system applies — planner should locate how it's wired (likely `scripts/` or a runner referenced from Railway config) and follow the established pattern.

### Logging — `system_logs` constraint

Existing `system_logs.valid_source_type` CHECK constraint permits only `orchestrator_gpu`, `orchestrator_api`, and `worker`. **Do not invent a new `source_type` value.** Use `source_type='orchestrator_gpu'` with a metadata tag field (e.g., `metadata->>'shadow_intended_action' = 'true'`) to distinguish shadow log entries from authoritative ones. Saves a migration and avoids breaking other readers of `system_logs`.

### New Python module — `gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_reconciler.py`

Owns the full five-phase state machine. Pure-where-possible, side-effects concentrated at boundaries.

```python
class CapacityReconciler:
    def reconcile(self, cycle_id: int) -> ReconciliationResult:
        observed = self.observe()                     # phase 1: read workers + tasks
        plan = self.plan(observed)                    # phase 2: compute CapacityPlan
        admitted = self.admit(plan)                   # phase 3: filter by backoff/cooldown
        result = self.act(admitted, shadow=self.shadow_mode)  # phase 4: side effects (or log-only)
        self.record(cycle_id, observed, plan, admitted, result)  # phase 5: persist intent row
        return result
```

Where:

- **`observe()`** is the canonical read of `workers` + `tasks` state. Counts active / spawning / route-stale / idle, computes `total_tasks = queued + in_progress`. Returns a frozen `Observed` dataclass — **immutable**, taken at one moment in time, passed by value through subsequent phases.
- **`plan(observed)`** computes ONE `desired_capacity` from ONE formula. Returns a `CapacityPlan` with `desired`, `effective_capacity = active + spawning - admitted_pending_cancels`, `actions = [Spawn | CancelPendingSpawn | TerminateIdle | Noop]`, `reason`, `valid_until`.
- **`admit(plan)`** is admission control. Checks `next_spawn_allowed_at` per pool, drops `Spawn` actions during cooldown, logs `suppressed_by_backoff`.
- **`act(admitted, shadow)`** executes side effects when `shadow=False`. When `shadow=True`, logs intended actions to `system_logs` (source_type=`orchestrator_gpu`, metadata tag `shadow_intended_action`). **In shadow mode, `act()` MUST NOT call RunPod, MUST NOT mutate worker rows, MUST NOT update backoff state. Shadow is read-only on the external world.**
- **`record(...)`** writes one row to `worker_capacity_intents` regardless of mode. The `shadow` boolean column distinguishes shadow rows from authoritative rows.

### Rules the reconciler MUST honor (invariants)

These are invariants, not preferences. The cycle-trace simulation tests in the deliverables section exist to enforce them.

1. **Spawning workers count as capacity** until they (a) report `spawn_failed`, (b) exceed `active_initializing_max_sec`, or (c) are explicitly cancelled by a `CancelPendingSpawn` action emitted by this reconciler.
2. **Never kill or cancel a spawning worker because `queued == 0`.** The queue snapshot does not have authority over an authorized spawn.
3. **Cancel `Spawn` action emission** only when the desired_capacity has remained ≤ effective for **at least two consecutive observed reconciler snapshots spanning at least `2 * orchestrator_poll_sec` of wall-clock time**, OR the previous intent's `valid_until` has elapsed. Wall-clock based, not iteration-count based — duplicate Railway replicas or delayed cycles make raw cycle count unreliable.
4. **Scale down active workers** only via idle timeout + min/buffer constraints. Active workers are never killed by capacity-pressure logic.
5. **RunPod `spawn_failed` backoff is keyed by `(pool, route_key)`**, not globally. `consecutive_spawn_failures += 1` and `next_spawn_allowed_at = now() + min(2^N * 30s, 600s)`. Successful spawn for that `(pool, route_key)` resets the counter to 0.
6. **`record()` writes exactly one row per pool per cycle**, even when nothing changed. Audit trail integrity.
7. **No authoritative spawn action executes without a prior `RECORD` write succeeding** within the same reconciliation. This means `act(shadow=False)` calls `record()` first and only then dispatches RunPod creates / cancellations. Idempotency: if `act()` partially completes and the process crashes, the next cycle sees the recorded intent and can resume.
8. **Single-writer per pool per cycle in authoritative mode.** Use a Postgres advisory lock keyed on `hashtext(pool)` or a lease row in a small `orchestrator_leases` table, held for the duration of `admit()` + `act()`. Without this, two Railway replicas can each spawn against the same pool's intent and double-provision. (Shadow mode does not need the lock — multiple shadows are fine since they don't mutate.)
9. **At cutover, the reconciler must adopt pre-existing spawning workers** that have no associated intent row. These were spawned by the legacy controller before cutover. Treat them as part of effective_capacity; assign a synthetic adoption intent on first observe, do not cancel them.

### Phasing — what gets deleted, what survives

**Deleted:**
- `_handle_early_termination` (the entire phase 2 block in `control_loop.py` at lines ~340-400).
- The `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` mixin tree (test-only duplicate code path). Includes any tests that exercised it; replace those with tests against the new reconciler.

**Survives:**
- Worker lifecycle probing (`_handle_spawning_workers`, heartbeat checks, promote-to-active logic) — stays in `control_loop.py`.
- RunPod pod creation primitives (`runpod_client.create_pod` and friends) — stay. `act()` calls into them.
- `min_active_gpus`, `machines_to_keep_idle`, `buffer_based` calculations — stay as **inputs** to `plan()`. **Out of scope for this sprint** is simplifying or renaming them; `plan()` consumes them and emits one authoritative `desired` from one formula. The planner MUST write down exactly that composition (what's the precedence order between `min_active_gpus` floor, `queued_coverage`, and `busy_buffer`?) in the plan output, and gate must verify it's specified.

---

## Rollout strategy — shadow mode then cutover

This is a production control loop with money burn and destructive actions. Cutover happens behind a feature flag, gated on observed divergence behavior.

### Phase A — Shadow deployment (cycle ordering matters)

- Reconciler ships with `shadow_mode=True` (env var: `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE=shadow|authoritative`).
- **Cycle ordering — explicit, not implicit:** at the start of each orchestrator cycle, after lifecycle probing (`_handle_spawning_workers` etc.) but **before** the legacy `_handle_early_termination` and `_calculate_scaling_decision`, capture an immutable `Observed` snapshot. Pass that frozen snapshot to the shadow reconciler. Let the legacy controller proceed with its normal logic on its own snapshot. Compare outcomes afterward.

  This ordering is non-negotiable: if the shadow runs after legacy mutations, divergence data is contaminated by what legacy already did, and the audit becomes meaningless.

- Shadow does `OBSERVE → PLAN → ADMIT → RECORD`. **It does NOT call `act()` in shadow mode.** Intent rows are written with `shadow=true`.
- Existing two-controller path continues to be authoritative. Nothing user-facing changes.
- A divergence audit query becomes the validation surface:
  ```sql
  -- Shadow's intended actions per cycle vs. legacy's actual outcomes per cycle, last 24h
  SELECT
    sl.created_at::date,
    sl.metadata->>'pool' AS pool,
    sl.metadata->>'shadow_intended_action' AS shadow_action,
    -- join against actual worker-row mutations from legacy controller
    ...
  FROM system_logs sl
  WHERE sl.source_type = 'orchestrator_gpu'
    AND sl.metadata ? 'shadow_intended_action'
    AND sl.created_at > now() - interval '24 hours';
  ```
  (Planner should write the full join against the `workers` table mutations or against a parallel `legacy_actions` log if one exists.)

### Phase B — Cutover decision criteria

Cutover from shadow to authoritative requires ALL of:

1. **≥24 hours of shadow mode in production traffic AND ≥N meaningful capacity events.** "Meaningful event" = a cycle where shadow_desired ≠ legacy_action OR shadow proposed `CancelPendingSpawn` OR backoff suppressed a spawn. N = at least 50; if 24h of normal traffic does not produce 50 such events, extend shadow window until they accumulate.
2. **Replay validation against captured production state.** Before cutover, the planner must produce a replay test that loads a captured snapshot of `workers` + `tasks` from the actual 2026-05-13 incident window (or a similar pathological window) and runs the reconciler against it. Assert: at the moment `queued` momentarily drops to 0 with active work in flight, the reconciler does NOT emit `CancelPendingSpawn` for the spawning worker. This is the **non-negotiable proof of correctness**.
3. **No divergence in scenarios where the legacy controller's behavior is correct.** If shadow says spawn while legacy correctly says terminate-idle on a genuinely-idle worker, that's a reconciler bug, not a legacy bug — fix and re-shadow.

### Phase C — Cutover

- Flip `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE=authoritative`. Reconciler becomes authoritative; legacy controller becomes a no-op stub kept for one more day.
- **At the moment of flag flip**, the reconciler's first OBSERVE will discover spawning workers with no prior intent row (they were spawned by legacy). Apply rule 9 above (synthetic adoption intent).
- Watch `scaling-audit --hours 6` output for 4 hours post-cutover. Effective utilisation should rise from baseline.
- 24h later: delete legacy stub, delete shadow-mode logging, delete the duplicate mixin code path.

---

## Deliverables (acceptance criteria)

The plan must produce, and execute must ship:

1. **Migration:** `worker_capacity_intents` table + indexes in `sql/20260514000000_create_worker_capacity_intents.sql`, registered with the orchestrator's existing migration system.
2. **`gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_reconciler.py`** + supporting modules (`capacity_plan.py` for pure PLAN computation, types/dataclasses for `CapacityPlan` / `Action` / `Observed`).
3. **`_handle_early_termination` deleted** from `control_loop.py`. Verified by grep returning zero matches.
4. **`gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` mixin tree deleted** (and any tests that exercised it removed or rewritten against the new reconciler).
5. **Single-writer lease/lock mechanism** for authoritative mode (advisory lock or `orchestrator_leases` table; planner picks, defends choice in plan output).
6. **Cycle-trace simulation tests** under `tests/control/` covering:
   - **Test A — Queued snapshot gate:** Queued drops to zero mid-render while 1 active worker has 5 in-progress segments and a spawning replacement is in flight. Reconciler must NOT emit `CancelPendingSpawn`. Spawning worker survives.
   - **Test B — Spawn backoff with fake clock:** Use a fake/frozen clock. RunPod returns `spawn_failed` 4 times in succession. Assert exact `next_spawn_allowed_at` after each failure (30s, 60s, 120s, 240s). After the clock advances past `next_spawn_allowed_at`, a fifth attempt succeeds and `consecutive_spawn_failures` resets to 0. **Required: a `Clock` injection seam** in the reconciler — no `datetime.now()` called directly inside `plan()` or `admit()`.
   - **Test C — Time-based hysteresis on cancel:** Spawning worker has been spawning for 250s when `desired_capacity` drops from 2 to 1. Reconciler must NOT immediately cancel. Cancellation may only emit when desired ≤ effective has held for ≥ `2 * orchestrator_poll_sec` of wall-clock time across at least two snapshots, OR the previous intent's `valid_until` has elapsed. Test both branches.
   - **Test D — Route-stale workers:** A worker with `is_route_stale=True` (per `worker_state.py:83` definition based on `worker_contract_version` and route contract compatibility) is present in the worker list. Reconciler must NOT count it toward `effective_capacity` when satisfying a fresh-route `desired`.
   - **Test E — Cutover adoption:** Pre-existing spawning worker rows with no intent-table predecessor are correctly adopted on first authoritative `observe()`. They are not cancelled. A synthetic adoption intent row is written.
   - **Test F — Replay against captured incident state:** Load fixture state representing the 2026-05-13 14:11-14:14 window (or equivalent synthetic pathological state). Run reconciler. Assert no oscillation, no premature cancel.
7. **Shadow-mode infrastructure:** `shadow_mode` flag, immutable `Observed` snapshot captured at the correct point in the cycle (before legacy capacity actions), shadow logging via `source_type='orchestrator_gpu'` + metadata tag, divergence audit SQL committed to `scripts/` or `sql/`.
8. **`scaling-audit` debug command extended** in `reigh-app/scripts/debug.py` to read `worker_capacity_intents` and report on shadow vs. authoritative cycles, divergence counts, and per-pool intent stability. Cross-repo touch; small and well-scoped.
9. **Feature-flag cutover plumbing:** `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE` env var controls shadow vs. authoritative. No-op stub of legacy controller for 24h post-cutover.

---

## Out of scope (do not touch in this sprint)

- **Cross-pool intent reasoning.** Each pool reconciled independently. Cross-pool rebalancing is a separate brief.
- **Simplifying `min_active_gpus` / `machines_to_keep_idle` / `buffer_based`.** Stays as input to `plan()`. The plan output must *specify* the composition (precedence order), but not change the input semantics.
- **UI / dashboard for the intent table.** `SELECT` queries are the interface.
- **Worker-side code (`reigh-worker/`).** Untouched.
- **App-side code (`reigh-app/`).** Only the cross-repo touch is extending `scaling-audit` debug command, which is small and well-scoped.
- **Replacing the existing config/feature-flag system.** Use whatever's already there.
- **Flipping production to the `control/phases/` mixin path.** Out of scope. The mixins are being deleted, not promoted.

---

## Watch items (gate signals)

The gate phase MUST verify each of these before allowing execute to proceed:

- **W1: Concurrency on `worker_capacity_intents` writes.** Multiple orchestrator replicas could exist (Railway autoscaling). Planner must specify uniqueness strategy: `UNIQUE (pool, cycle_id)` constraint, advisory lock around the write, or accept duplicates and let queries dedupe. Gate verifies the choice is defensible.
- **W2: Cutover sequencing.** What happens to in-flight spawning workers at the moment of `shadow_mode=False` flip? Rule 9 specifies adoption; gate verifies the implementation actually does it.
- **W3: Duplicate code path deletion completeness.** Verify by grep that `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` is removed AND no production code path imports from it AND any test rewrites are passing.
- **W4: Idempotency under cycle restart.** If `act()` partially completes (spawned one of two pods, then process crashes), the next cycle must not double-spawn. Rule 7 addresses this; gate verifies the implementation.
- **W5: Test fidelity.** Test F's captured-state fixture must be realistic. Planner should examine actual `workers` + `tasks` snapshots from the incident window if available, or synthetic state that reproduces the failure mode.
- **W6: Single-writer semantics.** Verify the advisory lock or lease implementation prevents two Railway replicas from acting on the same pool's intent simultaneously. Test by spawning two reconciler instances in the test harness against the same DB.
- **W7: Migration registration.** Verify the new migration in `sql/` is wired into whatever runner the orchestrator uses (likely a script invoked at Railway deploy time or referenced from `railway.json`). Migration must be idempotent.
- **W8: Cutover adoption test passes.** Test E above must pass before any cutover plan is signed off. Pre-existing spawning workers must not be orphaned or cancelled.

---

## Risk: what would falsify the architectural premise

If during prep or plan the planner discovers that the "two formulas computing different `desired`" diagnosis is wrong — e.g., the early-term and scale-up phases actually coordinate correctly through some mechanism this brief overlooked — **stop and report**. Do not proceed with deleting `_handle_early_termination` based on a faulty premise.

Less drastically: if the planner finds that the `CapacityPlan` abstraction can't cleanly express some real-world scenario in the existing `worker_state.py` decision logic (around lines 588-780), surface that during critique. The right answer might be a different decomposition than OBSERVE/PLAN/ADMIT/ACT/RECORD.

The whole point of running this at `high` author depth is so the planner can push back on the brief's design if it's wrong.

---

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Unify Capacity Control via Durable-Intent Reconciler

## Overview

The orchestrator currently runs two parallel capacity controllers per cycle: `_handle_early_termination` (control_loop.py:343–427) computes a "down‑scale" desired figure and terminates spawning workers; later, `_calculate_scaling_decision` → `calculate_scaling_decision_pure` (worker_state.py:556–676) computes a separate "up‑scale" desired and spawns new workers. The two formulas operate on overlapping state but emit opposing actions, producing the 4 %‑utilisation churn observed on 2026‑05‑13.

This plan replaces both controllers with one durable‑intent reconciler (`OBSERVE → PLAN → ADMIT → ACT → RECORD`) backed by a new `worker_capacity_intents` table, ships behind a shadow/authoritative feature flag, and folds RunPod `spawn_failed` backoff into the reconciler's admission phase. The mixin tree under `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` is confirmed test‑only (verified by grep: production imports are only in `main.py` → `control_loop.py`; mixin imports appear only in `tests/`) and is deleted in the same sprint.

Key shape of the repo:
- Production runtime: `gpu_orchestrator/main.py` → `OrchestratorControlLoop.run_single_cycle()` in `gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py:89`.
- Pure scaling math: `gpu_orchestrator/worker_state.py:556` (`calculate_scaling_decision_pure`).
- Config: `gpu_orchestrator/config.py:43` (`OrchestratorConfig`).
- Migration runner: `scripts/apply_sql_migrations.py` (hard‑codes the file list — must be updated; **no auto‑migrate on Railway boot** — `railway.json` doesn't run it).
- `system_logs.valid_source_type` CHECK constraint at `sql/20250115000000_create_system_logs.sql:30` restricts to `orchestrator_gpu | orchestrator_api | worker`.

Scope is intentionally tight: leave worker lifecycle probing, RunPod primitives, and `min_active_gpus` / `machines_to_keep_idle` / `buffer_based` semantics in place — `plan()` consumes them but emits one authoritative `desired`.

---

## Phase 1: Foundation — Schema, Config, Types

### Step 1: Add the `worker_capacity_intents` migration (`sql/20260514000000_create_worker_capacity_intents.sql`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** the migration file with the schema from the brief, using `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` and `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` for idempotency.
2. **Add** `UNIQUE (pool, cycle_id)` (W1 — single‑writer guard) and a partial index for `(pool, next_spawn_allowed_at) WHERE next_spawn_allowed_at IS NOT NULL` to make backoff reads cheap.
3. **Wire** the file into `scripts/apply_sql_migrations.py` (the hardcoded list at lines 39–44) so `setup_database.py` / on‑demand applies pick it up. Confirm idempotency by running the script twice locally against a throwaway schema.
4. **Do NOT** add a new value to `system_logs.valid_source_type`. Continue using `source_type='orchestrator_gpu'` and tag shadow rows via `metadata->>'shadow_intended_action'` (brief §"Logging").

### Step 2: Extend `OrchestratorConfig` for the reconciler (`gpu_orchestrator/config.py:43`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** fields (loaded in `from_env()` at line 114):
   - `capacity_reconciler_mode: str` — `"off" | "shadow" | "authoritative"` (env `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE`, default `"off"` initially, will flip to `"shadow"` after one deploy).
   - `spawn_failure_backoff_base_sec: int` (default 30), `spawn_failure_backoff_max_sec: int` (default 600).
   - `cancel_hysteresis_min_seconds: int` — default `2 * orchestrator_poll_sec`; keep wall‑clock based per invariant 3.
2. **Log** the new fields in `log_config()` (line 214).

### Step 3: Create reconciler types (`gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_types.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Define** frozen dataclasses:
   - `Observed` — `(cycle_id, taken_at, pool, route_key, active, spawning, idle, busy, queued, in_progress, route_stale_workers, workers_by_id, …)`. Frozen, immutable, passed by value.
   - `Action` (sealed): `Spawn(pool, route_key, count, reason)`, `CancelPendingSpawn(worker_id, reason)`, `TerminateIdle(worker_id, reason)`, `Noop(reason)`.
   - `CapacityPlan` — `(desired, effective_capacity, actions, reason, valid_until, stable_since)`.
   - `BackoffState` — `(consecutive_spawn_failures, next_spawn_allowed_at)`.
   - `ReconciliationResult` — `(plan, admitted_actions, suppressed, intent_row_id, mode)`.
2. **Define** a `Clock` protocol (`now() -> datetime`) — gating Test B (W4 / brief §Test B). Production `SystemClock` calls `datetime.now(timezone.utc)`; tests inject `FakeClock`.
3. **Document** `route_key` derivation: `f"{worker_backend}:{worker_profile}:{selector_namespace}:{selector_version or 'default'}:v{worker_contract_version}"` — derived from `OrchestratorConfig` for the orchestrator's currently selected route, and from `DerivedWorkerState` fields for each worker (used by `is_route_stale`‑aware accounting in `effective_capacity`).

---

## Phase 2: Reconciler Implementation

### Step 4: Pure PLAN computation (`gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_plan.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** `compute_plan(observed: Observed, prior_intent: Intent | None, backoff: BackoffState, config, clock) -> CapacityPlan` as a pure function (no I/O, no `datetime.now`).
2. **Single `desired` formula** — explicitly document the precedence (W brief §"Out of scope" requires this be written down):
   ```
   workers_covering_tasks = idle + spawning_fresh_route   # spawning workers count until they fail
   uncovered_tasks        = max(0, queued - workers_covering_tasks)
   spawn_for_tasks        = uncovered_tasks
   floor                  = max(min_active_gpus, busy + max(machines_to_keep_idle, 1))
   capacity_after_tasks   = current_capacity + spawn_for_tasks
   spawn_for_floor        = max(0, floor - capacity_after_tasks) if spawning_fresh_route == 0 else 0
   min_gap                = max(0, min_active_gpus - capacity_after_tasks)
   spawn_for_floor        = max(spawn_for_floor, min_gap)
   desired                = min(current_capacity + spawn_for_tasks + spawn_for_floor, max_active_gpus)
   effective_capacity     = active_fresh + spawning_fresh - cancels_in_admit
   ```
3. **Cancel/terminate semantics (invariants 2, 3, 4)**:
   - **Never** emit `CancelPendingSpawn` when `queued > 0` (invariant 2).
   - **Never** emit `TerminateIdle` for active workers under capacity pressure — only via idle timeout, handled by the existing `_execute_scaling` path which we keep for active‑worker idle termination (invariant 4).
   - Emit `CancelPendingSpawn` only when **all** hold: (a) `desired <= effective_capacity - 1` (genuine overshoot), (b) `prior_intent is not None and (clock.now() - prior_intent.stable_since) >= cancel_hysteresis_min_seconds`, (c) at least two consecutive prior observations also showed `desired <= effective` (encoded by checking `prior_intent.desired_capacity <= prior_intent.observed_active + prior_intent.observed_spawning`), OR `prior_intent.valid_until` has elapsed. Wall‑clock, not iteration count.
4. **Stale‑route workers** (invariant: don't count toward fresh `desired`) — partition `Observed.spawning` and `Observed.active` into `_fresh_route` vs `_stale_route`; only fresh count toward `effective_capacity` for a fresh `desired`.
5. **Return** `CapacityPlan(desired, effective_capacity, actions=[...], reason, valid_until=now + 2*poll_sec, stable_since)`.

### Step 5: Reconciler service (`gpu_orchestrator/control/capacity_reconciler.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** `CapacityReconciler` with the five‑phase `reconcile(cycle_id)` from the brief:
   - `observe()` — async; reads workers + task counts from `DatabaseClient`. Returns frozen `Observed`. Snapshot is taken once; subsequent phases never re‑read DB state for capacity decisions.
   - `plan(observed)` — calls `compute_plan` (Step 4). Loads `prior_intent` for the pool from `worker_capacity_intents` via `db.get_latest_intent(pool)`.
   - `admit(plan)` — drops `Spawn` actions when `clock.now() < backoff.next_spawn_allowed_at` for the `(pool, route_key)`; logs each suppression with `metadata={'suppressed_by_backoff': true, 'next_allowed_at': ...}`.
   - `act(admitted, shadow)`:
     - **Shadow mode:** log intended actions via `system_logs` with `source_type='orchestrator_gpu'` and `metadata={'shadow_intended_action': true, 'action_type': ..., 'pool': ..., 'route_key': ..., ...}`. Do not call RunPod. Do not mutate workers. Do not update backoff state.
     - **Authoritative mode:** call `record()` FIRST (invariant 7 — idempotency); only then call `self.runpod.create_pod(...)` for `Spawn` and `self.runpod.terminate_worker(...)` for `CancelPendingSpawn`. On RunPod failure, increment `consecutive_spawn_failures` and write back to the intent row. On success, reset to 0.
   - `record(...)` — single `INSERT` into `worker_capacity_intents` with the cycle's snapshot, plan, and backoff state. Honors invariant 6 (one row per pool per cycle, even when unchanged) and invariant 9 (synthetic adoption: if an observed spawning worker has no prior intent linkage at cutover, write a synthetic adoption intent on first authoritative cycle).
2. **Single‑writer lock for authoritative mode (W6, invariant 8):** use a Postgres advisory lock — wrap `admit()` + `act()` in `SELECT pg_try_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended(pool, 0))` via a short transaction. If the lock is not acquired, log `single_writer_skipped` and exit the cycle for that pool (the next cycle on the other replica will retry). Defended choice: no extra table, transaction‑scoped (auto‑released on crash), keyed per‑pool so different pools can advance in parallel. Shadow mode skips the lock.

### Step 6: Database access for intents (`gpu_orchestrator/database.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** thin methods on `DatabaseClient`:
   - `get_latest_intent(pool: str) -> dict | None`
   - `insert_capacity_intent(row: dict) -> uuid`
   - `update_intent_backoff(pool: str, route_key: str, consecutive_failures: int, next_allowed_at: datetime) -> None` — implemented as another `INSERT` into `worker_capacity_intents` (table is append‑only audit; "current state" is the latest row by `(pool, created_at DESC)`).
2. **Use** existing Supabase client patterns from neighbouring methods. Keep return types as dicts to match the rest of the module.

---

## Phase 3: Wire Into the Control Loop (Shadow Mode)

### Step 7: Capture immutable snapshot and run shadow reconciler (`gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **In `__init__`** (line 44), instantiate `CapacityReconciler(self.db, self.config, clock=SystemClock())` when `config.capacity_reconciler_mode in {"shadow", "authoritative"}`.
2. **In `run_single_cycle`** (line 89), after Phase 2 (`_derive_all_worker_states`, line 113) and BEFORE Phase 3 `_handle_early_termination` and Phase 8 `_calculate_scaling_decision`, build the frozen `Observed` snapshot from `worker_states` + `task_counts`. This ordering is non‑negotiable (brief §"Phase A cycle ordering"): observe before any legacy mutation, so shadow divergence data is uncontaminated.
3. **Invoke** `await self.reconciler.reconcile(self.cycle_count, observed=...)` regardless of mode. In `"shadow"` mode, legacy phases 3 and 8 continue to run authoritatively. In `"authoritative"` mode, the reconciler's `act()` performs the spawns/cancels.

### Step 8: Delete legacy capacity logic when authoritative (`gpu_orchestrator/control_loop.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **In `"authoritative"` mode**, gate the legacy controllers off:
   - Skip `_handle_early_termination` call at line 116.
   - In `_calculate_scaling_decision` / `_execute_scaling` (lines 802 and 943), retain ONLY the idle‑timeout termination path for active workers (invariant 4 — active‑worker scale‑down stays governed by idle timeout, not the reconciler). All `workers_to_spawn` / `workers_to_terminate` of spawning workers is driven by the reconciler.
2. **Phase D — full deletion (separate commit, after 24h authoritative bake):** remove `_handle_early_termination` (`control_loop.py:343–427`) entirely and remove the spawn paths from `_execute_scaling`. Brief requires `grep '_handle_early_termination' gpu_orchestrator/ | wc -l == 0` after the bake — this commit lives in the same PR but behind a clear commit boundary so it can be reverted independently if 24h of authoritative running surfaces a regression.

### Step 9: Delete the test‑only mixin tree (`gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Verify** zero production importers: `grep -rn 'control\.phases' gpu_orchestrator/` must return nothing. (Already confirmed — only `tests/` import the mixins.)
2. **Delete** the directory `gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/` and the test files under `tests/gpu_orchestrator/control/phases/`.
3. **Delete** mixin‑facing fixture tests that are now obsolete:
   - `tests/test_direct_control_health.py`
   - `tests/gpu_orchestrator/test_sprint7_staged_rollback_fixture.py`
   - `tests/gpu_orchestrator/test_sprint10_canary_rollback.py`
   - `tests/gpu_orchestrator/test_sprint12_staging_flip.py`
   Replace assertions worth keeping (if any) with equivalent assertions in the new `tests/control/` suite from Step 10.

---

## Phase 4: Tests and Tooling

### Step 10: Cycle‑trace simulation tests (`tests/control/test_capacity_reconciler.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Test A — Queued snapshot gate (brief §Test A):** synthesize `Observed` with `queued=0, in_progress=6, active=1 (busy), spawning=1`. Assert `compute_plan(...).actions` does NOT contain `CancelPendingSpawn`.
2. **Test B — Spawn backoff with `FakeClock` (brief §Test B):** drive `(pool, route_key)` through 4 simulated `spawn_failed` results. Assert `next_spawn_allowed_at` after each is exactly `clock.now() + {30, 60, 120, 240}` seconds. Advance clock past `next_spawn_allowed_at`, run a fifth attempt that succeeds, assert `consecutive_spawn_failures` resets to 0.
3. **Test C — Time‑based hysteresis (brief §Test C):** spawning worker created at `T=0`, observed at `T=250s`, `desired` drops `2 → 1`. With `prior_intent.stable_since = T-10s`, reconciler does NOT cancel. After advancing clock past `cancel_hysteresis_min_seconds`, cancel is emitted. Test both branches: hysteresis still open vs. `valid_until` already elapsed.
4. **Test D — Route‑stale workers (brief §Test D):** worker with `is_route_stale=True` does not count toward `effective_capacity` for fresh route; reconciler emits `Spawn` for the fresh route even though raw `len(spawning) > 0`.
5. **Test E — Cutover adoption (brief §Test E, invariant 9):** simulate pre‑existing spawning worker rows with no intent row. First authoritative `observe()` writes a synthetic adoption intent and the worker is NOT cancelled.
6. **Test F — Replay against captured incident state:** load a fixture (`tests/control/fixtures/incident_20260513_1411.json`) representing the queue=0 + 6 in‑progress + 1 spawning + 1 busy snapshot from the 2026‑05‑13 14:11–14:14 window. Synthetic if a real DB snapshot isn't available, but reproduce the exact failure mode. Assert no oscillation, no premature cancel across 5 simulated cycles spaced `poll_sec` apart. (W5)
7. **Test G — Single‑writer lock (W6):** spawn two `CapacityReconciler` instances against the same DB (use a shared transaction harness or a real Postgres test container if available); assert only one acquires the advisory lock per pool per cycle, the other no‑ops.
8. **Test H — Idempotency under crash (invariant 7, W4):** inject a failure between `record()` and `runpod.create_pod()`. Next cycle's `observe()` sees the prior intent row and does NOT double‑spawn.

### Step 11: Cross‑repo `scaling-audit` extension (`reigh-app/scripts/debug.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Extend** the existing `scaling-audit` subcommand (location confirmed by user/brief — not visible from this repo) to read `worker_capacity_intents`. Add columns: per‑pool intent stability (time between `desired_capacity` changes), shadow vs authoritative cycle counts in window, divergence counter (cycles where the shadow's intended action would have differed from what legacy actually did).
2. **Commit** the divergence audit SQL to `sql/audit_capacity_intent_divergence.sql` in this repo for portability. Include the join against `workers.updated_at` log entries that fall within the same `cycle_number`.

### Step 12: Validation
**Scope:** Small
1. **Targeted:** `pytest tests/control/ -v` (Tests A–H).
2. **Broader:** `pytest tests/ -v` — must still pass after mixin deletion.
3. **Migration smoke:** run `python scripts/apply_sql_migrations.py` against a throwaway database, twice, confirming idempotency.
4. **Shadow‑mode dry run:** boot the orchestrator locally with `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE=shadow` against a staging DB, run for ~10 cycles, assert (a) intent rows appear with `shadow=true`, (b) no RunPod calls fire from the reconciler, (c) legacy controllers continue to produce identical behavior to baseline.
5. **Authoritative dry run (staging only):** flip `ORCHESTRATOR_CAPACITY_RECONCILER_MODE=authoritative` in staging; verify the synthetic adoption path for any pre‑existing spawning workers (Test E in vivo).

---

## Execution Order

1. **Schema + types first** (Steps 1–3) — everything downstream depends on the migration and dataclasses.
2. **Pure PLAN logic** (Step 4) — testable without DB.
3. **Reconciler + DB methods** (Steps 5–6) — uses Step 4.
4. **Shadow wiring** (Step 7) — observable in prod with zero behavioral risk.
5. **Tests** (Step 10) — must pass BEFORE the legacy gate in Step 8.
6. **Legacy gate off + mixin deletion** (Steps 8–9) — only after Steps 4–7 are green and after divergence audit (brief §Phase B) clears.
7. **Cross‑repo audit tool** (Step 11) — can land in parallel with Step 7.
8. **Validation** (Step 12) at each phase boundary.

## Validation Order

1. Unit tests for `compute_plan` (no DB, no clock — fastest signal).
2. Integration tests for the reconciler (FakeClock, mock DB).
3. Two‑replica advisory‑lock test (Postgres‑backed).
4. Full repo `pytest` after mixin deletion.
5. Migration idempotency.
6. Shadow‑mode local boot.
7. Shadow‑mode staging — ≥24h, ≥50 meaningful events, brief §Phase B criteria.
8. Authoritative cutover in staging, then prod.
9. Effective‑utilisation metric ≥70% over 24h post‑cutover (the headline success criterion).

Each finding needs:
- "detail": what you specifically checked and what you found (at least a full sentence)
- "flagged": true if this describes a difference, risk, or tension — even if you think it's justified. false only if purely informational with no possible downside.
- Every check must end with at least one finding. Never leave a `findings` array empty. If you found no issue, add one detailed `flagged: false` finding explaining what you checked and why it appears clear.

When in doubt, flag it — the gate can accept tradeoffs, but it can't act on findings it never sees.

Good: {"detail": "Checked callers of nthroot_mod in solveset.py line 1205 — passes prime moduli only, consistent with the fix.", "flagged": false}
Good: {"detail": "The fix handles empty tuples but not single-element tuples which need a trailing comma.", "flagged": true}
Bad: {"detail": "No issue found", "flagged": false}  ← too brief, will be rejected
Bad: {"detail": "The hints suggest approach X but the plan uses Y. However Y is consistent with X's intent.", "flagged": false}  ← a different approach than the hints IS a flag. You found a divergence — flag it. The gate decides if it's acceptable.

After filling in checks, add any additional concerns to the `flags` array (e.g., security, performance, dependencies).
Use the standard format (id, concern, category, severity_hint, evidence). This array can be empty.

Workflow: read the file → investigate → read file again → add finding → write file back. Repeat for each check.

        Additional guidelines:
        - Robustness level: standard. Use balanced judgment. Flag significant risks, but do not spend flags on minor polish or executor-obvious boilerplate.
        - Over-engineering: prefer the simplest approach that fully solves the problem.
        - Reuse existing flag IDs when the same concern is still open.
        - `verified_flag_ids`: list flag IDs from prior iterations that the revised plan actually resolves (e.g., if the plan was revised to fix FLAG-001, and you confirm the fix is correct, include "FLAG-001"). Only include flags you've verified — don't guess.
        - Verify that the plan follows the expected structure when validator warnings or the outline suggest drift.
        - Additional flags may use these categories: correctness, security, completeness, performance, maintainability, other.
        - Focus on concrete issues, not structural formatting.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Apply SQL migrations to Supabase database.
This script applies the SQL files in the sql/ directory to create missing RPC functions.
"""

import os
import sys
from pathlib import Path
from dotenv import load_dotenv
from supabase import create_client, Client

def apply_sql_migrations():
    """Apply SQL migrations to create missing RPC functions."""
    
    # Load environment variables
    load_dotenv()
    
    # Get Supabase credentials
    supabase_url = os.getenv("SUPABASE_URL")
    supabase_key = os.getenv("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY")
    
    if not supabase_url or not supabase_key:
        print("❌ Error: SUPABASE_URL and SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY must be set in .env file")
        sys.exit(1)
    
    # Create Supabase client
    try:
        supabase: Client = create_client(supabase_url, supabase_key)
        print("✅ Connected to Supabase")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"❌ Failed to connect to Supabase: {e}")
        sys.exit(1)
    
    # Get SQL files directory
    sql_dir = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "sql"
    
    # SQL files to apply (in order)
    sql_files = [
        "20250202000000_add_missing_columns.sql",
        "20250202000001_create_rpc_functions_existing.sql", 
        "20250202000002_create_monitoring_views_existing.sql",
        "20250202000003_add_legacy_functions.sql"
    ]
    
    print("🔧 Applying SQL migrations...")
    
    for sql_file in sql_files:
        file_path = sql_dir / sql_file
        
        if not file_path.exists():
            print(f"⚠️  Warning: {sql_file} not found, skipping...")
            continue
            
        print(f"📄 Applying {sql_file}...")
        
        try:
            # Read SQL file
            with open(file_path, 'r', encoding='utf-8') as f:
                sql_content = f.read()

89	    async def run_single_cycle(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
90	        """
91	        Run a single orchestrator cycle.
92	        Returns summary of actions taken.
93	        """
94	        cycle_start = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
95	        self.cycle_count += 1
96	
97	        # Set cycle number in logging context

102	            pass
103	
104	        logger.debug("=== ORCHESTRATOR CYCLE #%s ===", self.cycle_count)
105	
106	        summary = CycleSummary()
107	
108	        try:
109	            # Phase 1: Fetch current state
110	            workers, excluded_workers, task_counts, detailed_counts = await self._fetch_current_state()
111	
112	            # Phase 2: Derive state for all workers
113	            worker_states = await self._derive_all_worker_states(workers, task_counts.queued)
114

116	            worker_states = await self._handle_early_termination(worker_states, task_counts, summary)
117

119	            await self._handle_spawning_workers(worker_states, task_counts, summary)

122	            await self._health_check_active_workers(worker_states, task_counts, summary)
123

125	            await self._cleanup_error_workers(worker_states, summary)
126	
127	            # Phase 7: Reconcile orphaned tasks
128	            await self._reconcile_orphaned_tasks(worker_states, summary)
129	
130	            # Phase 8: Calculate scaling decision
131	            scaling = await self._calculate_scaling_decision(worker_states, task_counts)
132	
133	            # Phase 9: Execute scaling
134	            await self._execute_scaling(scaling, worker_states, task_counts, summary, detailed_counts)
135	
136	            # Phase 10: Periodic checks (storage, zombies). Cleanup paths
137	            # also see excluded workers so harness crashes can't leak GPUs.
138	            await self._run_periodic_checks(worker_states, excluded_workers, task_counts.queued, summary)
139	
140	        except Exception as e:
141	            logger.error(f"Error in orchestrator cycle: {e}")
142	            summary.error = str(e)
143

146	        logger.debug(f"Orchestrator cycle completed in {cycle_duration:.2f}s: {summary.to_dict()['actions']}")
147	
148	        # Health monitoring
149	        total_workers = len([ws for ws in worker_states if not ws.is_terminal]) if 'worker_states' in dir() else 0

152	            worker_count=total_workers,
153	            workers_spawned=summary.workers_spawned
154	        )

156	
157	        if self.cycle_count % 10 == 0:
158	            if not self.health_monitor.check_logging_health():
159	                logger.error("Database logging is degraded - check logs above")
160	
161	        return {
162	            "timestamp": cycle_start.isoformat(),
163	            **summary.to_dict()
164	        }

167	    # PHASE 1: Fetch current state
168	    # =========================================================================
169	
170	    async def _fetch_current_state(self) -> Tuple[List[Dict], List[Dict], TaskCounts, Optional[Dict]]:
171	        """Fetch workers and task counts from database.
172	
173	        Returns ``(production_workers, excluded_workers, task_counts, detailed_counts)``.
174	
175	        Excluded workers (currently: live-test) bypass production capacity math
176	        and the spawn/health phases — but the periodic cleanup checks still see
177	        them, so harness crashes can't leak GPUs.
178	        """
179	        # Only fetch non-terminated workers. Terminated workers don't need
180	        # lifecycle checks, and including them bloats .in_() batch queries
181	        # past PostgREST URL limits, causing silent 400 errors.
182	        fetched_workers = await self.db.get_workers(status=["spawning", "active", "error"])
183	        workers, excluded_workers = partition_capacity_workers(fetched_workers)
184	        if excluded_workers:
185	            logger.info(
186	                "Ignoring %s worker(s) for production capacity control (cleanup still applies)",
187	                len(excluded_workers),
188	            )
189	
190	        # Get detailed task counts from edge function
191	        detailed_counts = await self.db.get_detailed_task_counts_via_edge_function()
192	
193	        if detailed_counts:
194	            totals = detailed_counts.get('totals', {})
195	            active_cloud_count = totals.get('active_only', 0)
196	            
197	            # NEW SCALING LOGIC: Use potentially_claimable for scaling decisions
198	            # potentially_claimable = queued_only + blocked_by_capacity
199	            # This excludes blocked_by_deps (uncertain) and blocked_by_settings (user choice)
200	            potentially_claimable = totals.get('potentially_claimable')
201	            
202	            if potentially_claimable is not None:
203	                # New edge function with breakdown - use potentially_claimable for scaling
204	                queued_count = potentially_claimable
205	                total_tasks = potentially_claimable + active_cloud_count
206	                
207	                # Log the breakdown for visibility
208	                queued_only = totals.get('queued_only', 0)  # Claimable right now (capacity-limited)
209	                blocked_capacity = totals.get('blocked_by_capacity', 0)
210	                blocked_deps = totals.get('blocked_by_deps', 0)
211	                blocked_settings = totals.get('blocked_by_settings', 0)
212	                
213	                logger.debug(
214	                    f"TASK COUNT (Cycle #{self.cycle_count}): "
215	                    f"Scaling={potentially_claimable} (queued_only={queued_only} + capacity_blocked={blocked_capacity}), "
216	                    f"Active={active_cloud_count}, "
217	                    f"[NOT scaling for: deps_blocked={blocked_deps}, settings_blocked={blocked_settings}]"
218	                )
219	            else:
220	                # Legacy edge function - fall back to queued_only
221	                queued_count = totals.get('queued_only', 0)
222	                total_tasks = totals.get('queued_plus_active', 0)
223	                logger.debug(
224	                    f"TASK COUNT (Cycle #{self.cycle_count}): "
225	                    f"Queued={queued_count}, Active={active_cloud_count}, Total={total_tasks}"
226	                )
227	            
228	            self._log_detailed_task_breakdown(detailed_counts, queued_count, active_cloud_count, total_tasks)
229	        else:
230	            logger.warning("Failed to get detailed task counts, falling back to simple counting")
231	            total_tasks = await self.db.count_available_tasks_via_edge_function(include_active=True)
232	            queued_count = await self.db.count_available_tasks_via_edge_function(include_active=False)
233	            active_cloud_count = total_tasks - queued_count
234	            logger.warning(f"Fallback returned: {total_tasks} total, {queued_count} queued")
235	
236	        logger.info(f"Cycle #{self.cycle_count}: scaling={queued_count} active={active_cloud_count}")
237	        return (
238	            workers,
239	            excluded_workers,
240	            TaskCounts(queued=queued_count, active_cloud=active_cloud_count, total=total_tasks),
241	            detailed_counts,
242	        )

340	    # PHASE 3: Early termination of over-capacity spawning workers
341	    # =========================================================================
342	
343	    async def _handle_early_termination(
344	        self,
345	        worker_states: List[DerivedWorkerState],
346	        task_counts: TaskCounts,
347	        summary: CycleSummary
348	    ) -> List[DerivedWorkerState]:
349	        """
350	        Terminate excess spawning workers before they finish starting up.
351	        Returns updated worker_states list with terminated workers removed.
352	        """
353	        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
354	        config = self.config
355	
356	        spawning_states = [ws for ws in worker_states if ws.is_spawning and not ws.is_route_stale]
357	        active_states = [
358	            ws for ws in worker_states
359	            if ws.is_active and not ws.is_terminal and not ws.is_route_stale
360	        ]
361	
362	        current_capacity = len(active_states) + len(spawning_states)
363	
364	        # Calculate early desired workers
365	        if task_counts.total > 0:
366	            down_scaled = math.ceil(task_counts.total * config.scale_down_multiplier)
367	            task_based = max(1, down_scaled)
368	        else:
369	            task_based = 0
370	
371	        early_desired = max(config.min_active_gpus, task_based, max(config.machines_to_keep_idle, 1))
372	        early_desired = min(early_desired, config.max_active_gpus)
373	
374	        if current_capacity <= early_desired:
375	            return worker_states
376	
377	        # Skip if there's queued work
378	        if task_counts.queued > 0:
379	            logger.debug("Skipping early termination because queued work exists")
380	            return worker_states
381	
382	        # Check grace period
383	        time_since_last_down = None
384	        if self.last_scale_down_at:
385	            time_since_last_down = (now - self.last_scale_down_at).total_seconds()
386	
387	        if time_since_last_down is not None and time_since_last_down < config.scale_down_grace_period_sec:
388	            logger.debug(f"Skipping early termination due to scale-down grace period: {time_since_last_down:.0f}s < {config.scale_down_grace_period_sec}s")
389	            return worker_states
390	
391	        # Find eligible spawning workers (past grace period)
392	        excess = current_capacity - early_desired
393	        eligible = []
394	        for ws in sorted(spawning_states, key=lambda x: x.created_at, reverse=True):
395	            age = (now - ws.created_at).total_seconds()
396	            if age > config.spawning_grace_period_sec:
397	                eligible.append(ws)
398	
399	        to_terminate = min(excess, len(eligible))
400	
401	        if to_terminate == 0:
402	            logger.debug("No spawning workers eligible for early termination (within grace period)")
403	            return worker_states
404	
405	        logger.info(f"Early termination: {current_capacity} workers > {early_desired} desired, terminating {to_terminate} spawning workers")
406	
407	        terminated_ids = set()
408	        for ws in eligible[:to_terminate]:
409	            if ws.runpod_id:
410	                success = await self.runpod.terminate_worker(ws.runpod_id)
411	                if success:
412	                    logger.info(f"Terminated spawning worker {ws.worker_id} (RunPod {ws.runpod_id})")
413	                else:
414	                    logger.warning(f"Failed to terminate spawning worker {ws.worker_id}")
415	
416	            metadata_update = {
417	                'termination_reason': f'early_termination_over_capacity ({current_capacity} > {early_desired})',
418	                'terminated_at': now.isoformat()
419	            }
420	            await self.db.update_worker_status(ws.worker_id, 'terminated', metadata_update)
421	            summary.workers_terminated += 1
422	            terminated_ids.add(ws.worker_id)
423	
424	        self.last_scale_down_at = now
425	
426	        # Return updated list without terminated workers
427	        return [ws for ws in worker_states if ws.worker_id not in terminated_ids]
428	
429	    # =========================================================================
430	    # PHASE 4: Handle spawning workers
431	    # =========================================================================
432	
433	    async def _handle_spawning_workers(
434	        self,
435	        worker_states: List[DerivedWorkerState],
436	        task_counts: TaskCounts,
437	        summary: CycleSummary
438	    ) -> None:
439	        """Handle pod initialization, script launch, and promotion."""

542	
543	def calculate_scaling_decision_pure(
544	    worker_states: list,  # List[DerivedWorkerState]
545	    task_counts: TaskCounts,
546	    config: 'OrchestratorConfig',
547	    failure_rate_ok: bool,
548	) -> ScalingDecision:
549	    """
550	    Pure function to calculate scaling decision with no I/O.
551	
552	    Separates reactive scaling (cover queued tasks) from proactive scaling
553	    (maintain buffer/minimum) to prevent double-spawning.
554	
555	    Args:
556	        worker_states: List of derived worker states
557	        task_counts: Current task counts
558	        config: Orchestrator configuration
559	        failure_rate_ok: Whether failure rate allows spawning
560	
561	    Returns:
562	        ScalingDecision with spawn/terminate recommendations
563	    """
564	    import math
565	
566	    # Count workers by state (exclude those marked for termination)
567	    active_states = [
568	        ws for ws in worker_states
569	        if ws.is_active and not ws.should_terminate and not ws.is_route_stale
570	    ]
571	    spawning_states = [
572	        ws for ws in worker_states
573	        if ws.is_spawning and not ws.should_terminate and not ws.is_route_stale
574	    ]
575	
576	    active_count = len(active_states)
577	    spawning_count = len(spawning_states)
578	    current_capacity = active_count + spawning_count
579	
580	    # Count idle vs busy among active workers
581	    idle_count = len([ws for ws in active_states if not ws.has_active_task])
582	    busy_count = active_count - idle_count
583	
584	    # =========================================================================
585	    # NEW SCALING LOGIC: Separate reactive from proactive
586	    # =========================================================================
587	
588	    # 1. Reactive scaling: Cover queued tasks
589	    # Workers that can take tasks: idle active workers + spawning workers
590	    workers_covering_tasks = idle_count + spawning_count
591	    uncovered_tasks = max(0, task_counts.queued - workers_covering_tasks)
592	    spawn_for_tasks = uncovered_tasks
593	
594	    # 2. Proactive scaling: Maintain floor (only if not already spawning)
595	    # Floor = max(min_active_gpus, busy_count + max(machines_to_keep_idle, 1))
596	    desired_floor = max(config.min_active_gpus, busy_count + max(config.machines_to_keep_idle, 1))
597	
598	    # Account for workers we're about to spawn for tasks
599	    capacity_after_task_spawns = current_capacity + spawn_for_tasks
600	
601	    # Only spawn for floor if no workers are currently spawning
602	    if spawning_count == 0:
603	        spawn_for_floor = max(0, desired_floor - capacity_after_task_spawns)
604	    else:
605	        spawn_for_floor = 0
606	
607	    # Exception: Always allow spawning to reach minimum (even if spawning in progress)
608	    min_gap = max(0, config.min_active_gpus - capacity_after_task_spawns)
609	    spawn_for_floor = max(spawn_for_floor, min_gap)
610	
611	    workers_to_spawn = spawn_for_tasks + spawn_for_floor
612	
613	    # =========================================================================
614	    # Legacy fields for backwards compatibility
615	    # =========================================================================
616	    total_workload = task_counts.total_workload
617	    if total_workload > 0:
618	        up_scaled = math.ceil(total_workload * config.scale_up_multiplier)
619	        task_based = max(1, up_scaled)
620	    else:
621	        task_based = 0
622	
623	    buffer_based = busy_count + max(config.machines_to_keep_idle, 1)
624	    min_based = config.min_active_gpus
625	
626	    desired = max(min_based, task_based, buffer_based)
627	    desired = min(desired, config.max_active_gpus)
628	
629	    # =========================================================================
630	    # Apply constraints
631	    # =========================================================================
632	
633	    # Don't spawn if failure rate is too high
634	    if not failure_rate_ok:
635	        workers_to_spawn = 0
636	
637	    # Don't spawn beyond max capacity
638	    max_spawnable = config.max_active_gpus - current_capacity
639	    workers_to_spawn = min(workers_to_spawn, max(0, max_spawnable))
640	
641	    # Update spawn_for_tasks and spawn_for_floor after constraints
642	    # (proportionally reduce if constrained)
643	    if workers_to_spawn < spawn_for_tasks + spawn_for_floor and workers_to_spawn > 0:
644	        # Prioritize tasks over floor
645	        actual_spawn_for_tasks = min(spawn_for_tasks, workers_to_spawn)
646	        actual_spawn_for_floor = workers_to_spawn - actual_spawn_for_tasks
647	    else:
648	        actual_spawn_for_tasks = spawn_for_tasks if workers_to_spawn > 0 else 0
649	        actual_spawn_for_floor = spawn_for_floor if workers_to_spawn > 0 else 0
650	
651	    # Calculate scale-down (if over capacity)
652	    workers_to_terminate = 0
653	    if current_capacity > desired:
654	        workers_to_terminate = current_capacity - desired
655	
656	    return ScalingDecision(
657	        desired_workers=desired,
658	        current_capacity=current_capacity,
659	        active_count=active_count,
660	        spawning_count=spawning_count,
661	        idle_count=idle_count,
662	        busy_count=busy_count,
663	        task_based_workers=task_based,
664	        buffer_based_workers=buffer_based,
665	        min_based_workers=min_based,
666	        workers_to_spawn=workers_to_spawn,
667	        workers_to_terminate=workers_to_terminate,
668	        failure_rate_ok=failure_rate_ok,
669	        at_max_capacity=current_capacity >= config.max_active_gpus,
670	        at_min_capacity=active_count <= config.min_active_gpus,
671	        # New observability fields
672	        spawn_reason_tasks=actual_spawn_for_tasks,
673	        spawn_reason_floor=actual_spawn_for_floor,
674	        uncovered_tasks=uncovered_tasks,
675	        workers_covering_tasks=workers_covering_tasks,
676	    )
677	
678	
679	@dataclass
680	class CycleSummary:
681	    """Summary of actions taken in a cycle."""
682	    workers_promoted: int = 0
683	    workers_failed: int = 0
684	    workers_spawned: int = 0
685	    workers_terminated: int = 0
686	    tasks_reset: int = 0
687	    startup_scripts_launched: int = 0
688	    error: Optional[str] = None
689	
690	    def to_dict(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
691	        return {
692	            "actions": {
693	                "workers_promoted": self.workers_promoted,
694	                "workers_failed": self.workers_failed,
695	                "workers_spawned": self.workers_spawned,
696	                "workers_terminated": self.workers_terminated,
697	                "tasks_reset": self.tasks_reset,
698	                "startup_scripts_launched": self.startup_scripts_launched,
699	            },
700	            "error": self.error,
701	        }

983	            to_terminate = min(len(terminatable), max_by_buffer, max_by_capacity, max_by_min_active)
984	
985	            if to_terminate > 0:
986	                logger.info(f"Terminating {to_terminate} workers (over-capacity: {decision.current_capacity} > {decision.desired_workers})")
987	
988	                for worker in terminatable[:to_terminate]:
989	                    reason = f"scale_down_idle (capacity {decision.current_capacity} > desired {decision.desired_workers})"
990	                    if await self._terminate_worker(worker, reason=reason):
991	                        summary.workers_terminated += 1
992	                        logger.info(f"Terminated idle worker {worker['id']} (idle > {dynamic_timeout}s)")
993	
994	        # Scale up: spawn new workers
995	        if decision.should_scale_up:
996	            logger.info(
997	                f"SCALING UP: Spawning {decision.workers_to_spawn} workers "
998	                f"(current: {decision.current_capacity}, desired: {decision.desired_workers})"
999	            )
1000	
1001	            # Log detailed task information that triggered the scale-up
1002	            if detailed_counts:
1003	                queued_tasks = detailed_counts.get('queued_tasks', [])
1004	                active_tasks = detailed_counts.get('active_tasks', [])
1005	
1006	                if queued_tasks:
1007	                    logger.debug(f"QUEUED TASKS triggering scale-up ({len(queued_tasks)} tasks):")
1008	                    for i, task in enumerate(queued_tasks[:10], 1):
1009	                        task_id = task.get('task_id', 'unknown')[:8]
1010	                        task_type = task.get('task_type', 'unknown')
1011	                        user_id = task.get('user_id', 'unknown')
1012	                        created_at = task.get('created_at', 'unknown')
1013	                        if created_at != 'unknown':
1014	                            created_at = created_at[11:19] if len(created_at) > 19 else created_at
1015	                        logger.debug(f"   {i}. {task_id}... | {task_type:25} | user: {user_id} | created: {created_at}")
1016	
1017	                    if len(queued_tasks) > 10:
1018	                        logger.debug(f"   ... and {len(queued_tasks) - 10} more queued tasks")
1019	
1020	                if active_tasks:
1021	                    logger.debug(f"ACTIVE TASKS currently being processed ({len(active_tasks)} tasks):")
1022	                    for i, task in enumerate(active_tasks[:5], 1):
1023	                        task_id = task.get('task_id', 'unknown')[:8]
1024	                        task_type = task.get('task_type', 'unknown')
1025	                        worker_id = task.get('worker_id', 'unknown')
1026	                        started_at = task.get('started_at', 'unknown')
1027	                        if started_at != 'unknown':
1028	                            started_at = started_at[11:19] if len(started_at) > 19 else started_at
1029	                        logger.debug(f"   {i}. {task_id}... | {task_type:25} | worker: {worker_id} | started: {started_at}")
1030	
1031	                    if len(active_tasks) > 5:
1032	                        logger.debug(f"   ... and {len(active_tasks) - 5} more active tasks")
1033	
1034	            logger.debug(f"SCALING UP: Creating {decision.workers_to_spawn} new workers")
1035	
1036	            for i in range(decision.workers_to_spawn):
1037	                if await self._spawn_worker(queued_count=task_counts.queued):
1038	                    summary.workers_spawned += 1
1039	                    logger.info(f"Worker {i + 1}/{decision.workers_to_spawn} spawned successfully")
1040	                else:
1041	                    logger.error(f"Failed to spawn worker {i + 1}/{decision.workers_to_spawn}")
1042	
1043	    async def _handle_stale_route_workers(
1044	        self,
1045	        worker_states: List[DerivedWorkerState],
1046	        summary: CycleSummary,
1047	    ) -> None:
1048	        """Drain active route-stale workers and terminate idle route-stale workers."""
1049	        for ws in worker_states:
1050	            if not ws.is_route_stale or ws.is_terminal:
1051	                continue
1052	            if ws.has_active_task:
1053	                logger.info(
1054	                    "Draining route-stale worker %s with active task; no new-route capacity credit",
1055	                    ws.worker_id,
1056	                )
1057	                continue
1058	
1059	            worker = await self.db.get_worker_by_id(ws.worker_id)
1060	            if not worker:
1061	                logger.warning("Route-stale worker %s disappeared before termination", ws.worker_id)
1062	                continue
1063	
1064	            reason = ws.termination_reason or "STALE_ROUTE_CONTRACT"
1065	            if not reason.startswith("STALE_ROUTE_CONTRACT"):
1066	                reason = f"STALE_ROUTE_CONTRACT: {reason}"
1067	            if await self._terminate_worker(worker, reason=reason):
1068	                summary.workers_terminated += 1
1069	                logger.info("Terminated idle route-stale worker %s", ws.worker_id)
1070	
1071	    def _selected_route_metadata(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
1072	        metadata_fn = getattr(self.runpod, "selected_route_metadata", None)
1073	        if callable(metadata_fn):
1074	            return metadata_fn()
1075	        return {}
1076	
1077	    # =========================================================================
1078	    # PHASE 10: Periodic checks
1079	    # =========================================================================

1386	                    final_status = 'active'
1387	                elif status == 'error':
1388	                    final_status = 'terminated'
1389	                else:
1390	                    final_status = 'spawning'

135	    def emit(self, record: logging.LogRecord):
136	        """
137	        Queue log record for batch processing.
138	        
139	        This is called by the logging framework when a log is emitted.
140	        We queue it for background processing to avoid blocking.
141	        """
142	        try:
143	            # Build log entry
144	            entry = {
145	                'timestamp': datetime.fromtimestamp(record.created, tz=timezone.utc).isoformat(),
146	                'source_type': self.source_type,
147	                'source_id': self.source_id,
148	                'log_level': record.levelname,
149	                'message': self._format_message(record),
150	                'metadata': {
151	                    'module': record.module,
152	                    'funcName': record.funcName,
153	                    'lineno': record.lineno,
154	                    'pathname': record.pathname,
155	                    'thread': record.thread,
156	                    'threadName': record.threadName,
157	                }
158	            }
159	            
160	            # Add context if available (from contextvars - async-safe)
161	            cycle_number = _context_cycle.get()
162	            if cycle_number is not None and cycle_number > 0:
163	                entry['cycle_number'] = cycle_number
164	            
165	            task_id = _context_task_id.get()
166	            if task_id:
167	                entry['task_id'] = task_id
168	            
169	            worker_id = _context_worker_id.get()
170	            if worker_id:
171	                entry['worker_id'] = worker_id
172	            
173	            # Add exception info if present
174	            if record.exc_info:
175	                import traceback
176	                entry['metadata']['exception'] = ''.join(
177	                    traceback.format_exception(*record.exc_info)
178	                )
179	            
180	            # Add extra fields from record
181	            if hasattr(record, 'extra_data'):
182	                entry['metadata']['extra'] = record.extra_data
183	            
184	            # Try to add to queue (non-blocking to avoid deadlock)
185	            try:
186	                self.log_queue.put_nowait(entry)
187	                self.total_logs_queued += 1
188	            except queue.Full:
189	                # Queue is full, drop log to prevent memory issues

18	class LifecyclePhaseMixin:

20	
21	    async def _handle_early_termination(
22	        self: "ControlHost",
23	        worker_states: List[DerivedWorkerState],
24	        task_counts: TaskCounts,
25	        summary: CycleSummary,
26	    ) -> List[DerivedWorkerState]:
27	        """
28	        Terminate excess spawning workers before they finish starting up.
29	        Returns updated worker_states list with terminated workers removed.
30	        """
31	        now = datetime.now(timezone.utc)
32	        config = self.config
33

35	        active_states = [
36	            ws for ws in worker_states
37	            if ws.is_active and not ws.is_terminal and not ws.is_route_stale
38	        ]
39	
40	        current_capacity = len(active_states) + len(spawning_states)

142	        load_dotenv()
143	        
144	        supabase_url = os.getenv("SUPABASE_URL")
145	        supabase_key = os.getenv("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY")
146	        
147	        if not supabase_url or not supabase_key:
148	            raise ValueError("SUPABASE_URL and SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY must be set")
149	        
150	        self.supabase: Client = create_client(supabase_url, supabase_key)
151	    
152	    # Task operations (minimal - most task management is done by headless workers)
153	    async def count_available_tasks_via_edge_function(self, include_active: bool = True) -> int:
154	        """
155	        Get available task count using the new task-counts endpoint.
156	        This ensures the orchestrator sees exactly the same task count as workers.
157	        
158	        Args:
159	            include_active: If True, includes both Queued and In Progress tasks.
160	                           If False, only includes Queued tasks.
161	        
162	        Returns:
163	            Number of available tasks that workers can see.
164	        """
165	        try:
166	            supabase_url = os.getenv("SUPABASE_URL")
167	            supabase_key = os.getenv("SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY")
168	            
169	            if not supabase_url or not supabase_key:
170	                logger.error("Missing Supabase edge-function configuration")
171	                return 0
172	            
173	            task_counts_url = f"{supabase_url}/functions/v1/task-counts"
174	            
175	            headers = {
176	                "Authorization": f"Bearer {supabase_key}",
177	                "Content-Type": "application/json"
178	            }
179	            
180	            payload = {
181	                "run_type": "gpu",
182	                "include_active": include_active,
183	                **selected_pool_route_filter(),
184	            }
185	            
186	            timeout = aiohttp.ClientTimeout(total=30)
187	            async with aiohttp.ClientSession(timeout=timeout) as session:
188	                async with session.post(task_counts_url, headers=headers, json=payload) as response:
189	                    if response.status == 200:
190	                        data = await response.json()
191	                        # Extract count from new endpoint format
192	                        if "totals" in data:
193	                            data = apply_selected_pool_totals(data)
194	                            totals = data.get("selected_pool_totals") or data["totals"]
195	                            task_count = totals.get("queued_plus_active" if include_active else "queued_only", 0)
196	                        else:
197	                            task_count = data.get("available_tasks", 0)
